id="S4.SS3.SSS2.p5.pic1" class="ltx_picture" /></span>

</div>

<div id="S4.SS3.SSS2.p6" class="ltx_para">

<span id="S4.SS3.SSS2.p6.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Impact of Scope of Modification: Function-, File-, and Multi-File Changes.</span> Figure <a href="#S4.F3" class="ltx_ref" title="Figure 3 ‣ IV-C Patch-Type Sensitivity Analysis (RQ3) ‣ IV Comparison and Evaluation ‣ Vulnerability-Affected Versions Identification: How Far Are We?"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">3</span></a> compares tool performance across different patch scopes. Both tracing-based and matching-based methods perform similarly on patches confined to a single function or multiple functions within the same file. However, their effectiveness drops significantly on *multi-file* patches. A closer inspection reveals that multi-file patches introduce substantially more non-vulnerability-related changes. In a sample of 50 patches from each category, multi-file patches (20) included significantly more non-vulnerability-related changes than single-file ones (7), adding noise and hindering accurate detection.

</div>

<div id="S4.SS3.SSS2.p7" class="ltx_para">

Among all tools, recall tends to increase while precision declines as the patch scope expands. This is due to coarse-grained analysis: tracing-based tools operate at the hunk level, while matching-based tools assess at the function level. A single matched function may cause an entire patch to be flagged, increasing recall but reducing precision.

</div>

<div id="S4.SS3.SSS2.p8" class="ltx_para">

In terms of performance, <span id="S4.SS3.SSS2.p8.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">Lifetime</span> achieves the highest F1 on *single-function* patches, while <span id="S4.SS3.SSS2.p8.1.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">V-SZZ</span> performs the best on both *single-file* and *multi-file* patches. The dataset includes 585 single-function (51.9%), 337 single-file (29.9%), and 206 multi-file patches (18.2%). Though multi-file patches are less frequent, they still constitute a substantial portion of real-world cases and remain a key challenge.

</div>

<div id="S4.SS3.SSS2.p9" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

<span class="ltx_inline-block"><img src="data:image/svg+xml;base64,PHN2ZyBpZD0iUzQuU1MzLlNTUzIucDkucGljMSIgY2xhc3M9Imx0eF9waWN0dXJlIiBoZWlnaHQ9IjcxLjU4IiBvdmVyZmxvdz0idmlzaWJsZSIgdmVyc2lvbj0iMS4xIiB2aWV3Ym94PSIwIDAgNDc3LjM4IDcxLjU4IiB3aWR0aD0iNDc3LjM4Ij48ZyBzdHlsZT0iLS1sdHgtc3Ryb2tlLWNvbG9yOiMwMDAwMDA7LS1sdHgtZmlsbC1jb2xvcjojMDAwMDAwOyIgZmlsbD0iIzAwMDAwMCIgc3Ryb2tlPSIjMDAwMDAwIiBzdHJva2Utd2lkdGg9IjAuNHB0IiB0cmFuc2Zvcm09InRyYW5zbGF0ZSgwLDcxLjU4KSBtYXRyaXgoMSAwIDAgLTEgMCAwKSI+PGcgc3R5bGU9Ii0tbHR4LWZpbGwtY29sb3I6I0U2RTZFNjsiIGZpbGw9IiNFNkU2RTYiIGZpbGwtb3BhY2l0eT0iMS4wIj48cGF0aCBzdHlsZT0ic3Ryb2tlOm5vbmUiIGQ9Ik0gMS45NyA1LjkxIEwgMS45NyA2NS42OCBDIDEuOTcgNjcuODUgMy43MyA2OS42MiA1LjkxIDY5LjYyIEwgNDcxLjQ3IDY5LjYyIEMgNDczLjY1IDY5LjYyIDQ3NS40MSA2Ny44NSA0NzUuNDEgNjUuNjggTCA0NzUuNDEgNS45MSBDIDQ3NS40MSAzLjczIDQ3My42NSAxLjk3IDQ3MS40NyAxLjk3IEwgNS45MSAxLjk3IEMgMy43MyAxLjk3IDEuOTcgMy43MyAxLjk3IDUuOTEgWiIgLz48L2c+PGcgZmlsbC1vcGFjaXR5PSIxLjAiIHRyYW5zZm9ybT0ibWF0cml4KDEuMCAwLjAgMC4wIDEuMCA4Ljg5IDcuNDMpIj48Zm9yZWlnbm9iamVjdCBzdHlsZT0iLS1sdHgtZm8td2lkdGg6MzMuMjJlbTstLWx0eC1mby1oZWlnaHQ6NC4yOWVtOy0tbHR4LWZvLWRlcHRoOjAuMTllbTtmb250LXNpemU6MTBwdDsiIGhlaWdodD0iNjIuMTEiIG92ZXJmbG93PSJ2aXNpYmxlIiB0cmFuc2Zvcm09Im1hdHJpeCgxIDAgMCAtMSAwIDU5LjQyKSIgd2lkdGg9IjQ1OS42NyI+PHNwYW4gY2xhc3M9Imx0eF9mb3JlaWdub2JqZWN0X2NvbnRhaW5lciI+PHNwYW4gY2xhc3M9Imx0eF9mb3JlaWdub2JqZWN0X2NvbnRlbnQiPgo8c3BhbiBpZD0iUzQuU1MzLlNTUzIucDkucGljMS4xIiBjbGFzcz0ibHR4X2lubGluZS1ibG9jayBsdHhfbWluaXBhZ2UgbHR4X2FsaWduX2JvdHRvbSIgc3R5bGU9IndpZHRoOjMzLjIyZW07Ij4KPHNwYW4gaWQ9IlM0LlNTMy5TU1MyLnA5LnBpYzEuMS4xIiBjbGFzcz0ibHR4X3AiPjxzcGFuIGlkPSJTNC5TUzMuU1NTMi5wOS5waWMxLjEuMS4xIiBjbGFzcz0ibHR4X3RleHQgbHR4X2ZvbnRfYm9sZCIgc3R5bGU9Ii0tbHR4LWZnLWNvbG9yOiMwMDAwMDA7Ij5GaW5kaW5nIDc6PHNwYW4gaWQ9IlM0LlNTMy5TU1MyLnA5LnBpYzEuMS4xLjEuMSIgY2xhc3M9Imx0eF90ZXh0IGx0eF9mb250X21lZGl1bSI+Ck11bHRpLWZpbGUgcGF0Y2hlcyBpbnRyb2R1Y2UgbW9yZSBpcnJlbGV2YW50IGNoYW5nZXMsIGxlYWRpbmcgdG8gbm90YWJsZSBwZXJmb3JtYW5jZSBkcm9wcyBhY3Jvc3MgdG9vbHMuIEFsdGhvdWdoIGxlc3MgY29tbW9uLCB0aGV5IHBvc2UgYSBzaWduaWZpY2FudCBjaGFsbGVuZ id="S4.SS3.SSS2.p9.pic1" class="ltx_picture" /></span>

</div>

<figure id="S4.F4" class="ltx_figure">
<div class="ltx_flex_figure">
<div class="ltx_flex_cell ltx_flex_size_2">
<figure id="S4.F4.sf1" class="ltx_figure ltx_figure_panel ltx_align_center">

<figcaption><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_figure">(a) </span>Single-Branch</figcaption>
</figure>
</div>
<div class="ltx_flex_cell ltx_flex_size_2">
<figure id="S4.F4.sf2" class="ltx_figure ltx_figure_panel ltx_align_center">

<figcaption><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_figure">(b) </span>Multi-Branch</figcaption>
</figure>
</div>
</div>
<figcaption><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_figure">Figure 4: </span>Performance of Different Tools under Single-Branch and Multi-Branch Projects.</figcaption>
</figure>

<div id="S4.SS3.SSS2.p10" class="ltx_para">

<span id="S4.SS3.SSS2.p10.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Impact of Cross-Branch: Single-branch vs Multi-branch.</span> Figure <a href="#S4.F4" class="ltx_ref" title="Figure 4 ‣ IV-C2 Results ‣ IV-C Patch-Type Sensitivity Analysis (RQ3) ‣ IV Comparison and Evaluation ‣ Vulnerability-Affected Versions Identification: How Far Are We?"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">4</span></a> summarizes tool performance under single-branch and multi-branch settings. In multi-branch scenarios most tools suffer marked performance declines, for instance, <span id="S4.SS3.SSS2.p10.1.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">VULTURE</span>’s F1-score plunges by 31.2% and <span id="S4.SS3.SSS2.p10.1.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">V-SZZ</span>’s accuracy drops 32.7%, highlighting the added complexity of identifying affected versions across diverging codebases. Among tracing-based tools, <span id="S4.SS3.SSS2.p10.1.4" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">V-SZZ</span> is the only one that incorporates cross-branch information during version inference. This strategy helps mitigate recall degradation in multi-branch settings. However, its multi-step tracing introduces noise from unrelated commits, which reduces precision and partially offsets the benefit. Other tracing tools rely solely on patches from the main branch and thus miss cross-branch vulnerabilities, leading to larger performance declines. Matching-based tools are more severely affected by multi-branch development due to increased structural variability across branches. Prior work \[<a href="#bib.bib6" class="ltx_ref">6</a>\] shows that only 9.37% of patches are syntactically identical across branches, limiting the effectiveness of exact or near-exact matching strategies used by tools such as <span id="S4.SS3.SSS2.p10.1.5" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">MOVERY</span> and <span id="S4.SS3.SSS2.p10.1.6" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">FIRE</span>. Overall, identifying affected versions in multi-branch projects remains a challenging task.

</div>

<div id="S4.SS3.SSS2.p11" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

<span class="ltx_inline-block"><img src="data:image/svg+xml;base64,PHN2ZyBpZD0iUzQuU1MzLlNTUzIucDExLnBpYzEiIGNsYXNzPSJsdHhfcGljdHVyZSIgaGVpZ2h0PSI3MS41OCIgb3ZlcmZsb3c9InZpc2libGUiIHZlcnNpb249IjEuMSIgdmlld2JveD0iMCAwIDQ3Ny4zOCA3MS41OCIgd2lkdGg9IjQ3Ny4zOCI+PGcgc3R5bGU9Ii0tbHR4LXN0cm9rZS1jb2xvcjojMDAwMDAwOy0tbHR4LWZpbGwtY29sb3I6IzAwMDAwMDsiIGZpbGw9IiMwMDAwMDAiIHN0cm9rZT0iIzAwMDAwMCIgc3Ryb2tlLXdpZHRoPSIwLjRwdCIgdHJhbnNmb3JtPSJ0cmFuc2xhdGUoMCw3MS41OCkgbWF0cml4KDEgMCAwIC0xIDAgMCkiPjxnIHN0eWxlPSItLWx0eC1maWxsLWNvbG9yOiNFNkU2RTY7IiBmaWxsPSIjRTZFNkU2IiBmaWxsLW9wYWNpdHk9IjEuMCI+PHBhdGggc3R5bGU9InN0cm9rZTpub25lIiBkPSJNIDEuOTcgNS45MSBMIDEuOTcgNjUuNjggQyAxLjk3IDY3Ljg1IDMuNzMgNjkuNjIgNS45MSA2OS42MiBMIDQ3MS40NyA2OS42MiBDIDQ3My42NSA2OS42MiA0NzUuNDEgNjcuODUgNDc1LjQxIDY1LjY4IEwgNDc1LjQxIDUuOTEgQyA0NzUuNDEgMy43MyA0NzMuNjUgMS45NyA0NzEuNDcgMS45NyBMIDUuOTEgMS45NyBDIDMuNzMgMS45NyAxLjk3IDMuNzMgMS45NyA1LjkxIFoiIC8+PC9nPjxnIGZpbGwtb3BhY2l0eT0iMS4wIiB0cmFuc2Zvcm09Im1hdHJpeCgxLjAgMC4wIDAuMCAxLjAgOC44OSA3LjQzKSI+PGZvcmVpZ25vYmplY3Qgc3R5bGU9Ii0tbHR4LWZvLXdpZHRoOjMzLjIyZW07LS1sdHgtZm8taGVpZ2h0OjQuMjllbTstLWx0eC1mby1kZXB0aDowLjE5ZW07Zm9udC1zaXplOjEwcHQ7IiBoZWlnaHQ9IjYyLjExIiBvdmVyZmxvdz0idmlzaWJsZSIgdHJhbnNmb3JtPSJtYXRyaXgoMSAwIDAgLTEgMCA1OS40MikiIHdpZHRoPSI0NTkuNjciPjxzcGFuIGNsYXNzPSJsdHhfZm9yZWlnbm9iamVjdF9jb250YWluZXIiPjxzcGFuIGNsYXNzPSJsdHhfZm9yZWlnbm9iamVjdF9jb250ZW50Ij4KPHNwYW4gaWQ9IlM0LlNTMy5TU1MyLnAxMS5waWMxLjEiIGNsYXNzPSJsdHhfaW5saW5lLWJsb2NrIGx0eF9taW5pcGFnZSBsdHhfYWxpZ25fYm90dG9tIiBzdHlsZT0id2lkdGg6MzMuMjJlbTsiPgo8c3BhbiBpZD0iUzQuU1MzLlNTUzIucDExLnBpYzEuMS4xIiBjbGFzcz0ibHR4X3AiPjxzcGFuIGlkPSJTNC5TUzMuU1NTMi5wMTEucGljMS4xLjEuMSIgY2xhc3M9Imx0eF90ZXh0IGx0eF9mb250X2JvbGQiIHN0eWxlPSItLWx0eC1mZy1jb2xvcjojMDAwMDAwOyI+RmluZGluZyA4OjxzcGFuIGlkPSJTNC5TUzMuU1NTMi5wMTEucGljMS4xLjEuMS4xIiBjbGFzcz0ibHR4X3RleHQgbHR4X2ZvbnRfbWVkaXVtIj4KTXVsdGktYnJhbmNoIGRldmVsb3BtZW50IHNpZ25pZmljYW50bHkgZGVncmFkZXMgdGhlIGVmZmVjdGl2ZW5lc3Mgb2YgYm90aCB0cmFjaW5nLWJhc2VkIGFuZCBtYXRjaGluZy1iYXNlZCB0b29scywgZHVlIHRvIGxpbWl0ZWQgY3Jvc3MtYnJhbmNoIHBhdGNoIHV0aWxpe </div>

</div>

</div>

<div id="S4.SS4" class="section ltx_subsection">

### <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsection"><span id="S4.SS4.6" class="ltx_text">IV-D</span> </span><span id="S4.SS4.7" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">Tool Combination Analysis (RQ4)</span>

<div id="S4.SS4.p1" class="ltx_para">

Based on earlier findings, this RQ investigates whether combining tool components or outputs can yield performance improvements over standalone tools. We explore this from two angles: modular recomposition of tracing-based tools and ensemble strategies spanning tracing- and matching-based tools.

</div>

<div id="S4.SS4.SSS1" class="section ltx_subsubsection">

#### <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsubsection"><span id="S4.SS4.SSS1.6" class="ltx_text">IV-D</span>1 </span><span id="S4.SS4.SSS1.7" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Setup</span>

<div id="S4.SS4.SSS1.p1" class="ltx_para">

Our evaluation is organized into two phases:

</div>

<div id="S4.SS4.SSS1.p2" class="ltx_para">

<span id="S4.SS4.SSS1.p2.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Phase-1: Modular Recomposition of Tracing-based Tools.</span> We focus on tracing-based tools because their modular workflows are amenable to recombination, whereas the core stages of matching-based tools are often tightly coupled and less separable. Based on the stage-wise decomposition in RQ2, we identify four stages: (S1) statement selection, (S2) impact range inference, (S3) commit tracing, and (S4) cross-branch patch reuse. Prior results show that LLM-based methods in S1 and patch propagation in S4 consistently outperform alternatives. Fixing these two stages, we systematically explore combinations of 2 alternatives in S2 and 4 in S3, yielding 7 hybrid configurations (${{2 \times 4} - 1} = 7$). The best variant (<span id="S4.SS4.SSS1.p2.1.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">LLM4SZZ+</span>) serves as a representative for Phase-2.

</div>

<div id="S4.SS4.SSS1.p3" class="ltx_para">

<span id="S4.SS4.SSS1.p3.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Phase-2: Cross-Tool Combination.</span> This phase investigates whether outputs from diverse tools—spanning both tracing- and matching-based paradigms—can be effectively integrated. We select ten high-performing tools (F1-score $\geq$ 70%): <span id="S4.SS4.SSS1.p3.1.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">VCCFinder</span>, <span id="S4.SS4.SSS1.p3.1.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">V-SZZ</span>, <span id="S4.SS4.SSS1.p3.1.4" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">Lifetime</span>, <span id="S4.SS4.SSS1.p3.1.5" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">SEM-SZZ</span>, <span id="S4.SS4.SSS1.p3.1.6" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">LLM4SZZ</span>, <span id="S4.SS4.SSS1.p3.1.7" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">ReDebug</span>, <span id="S4.SS4.SSS1.p3.1.8" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">Movery</span>, <span id="S4.SS4.SSS1.p3.1.9" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">FIRE</span>, <span id="S4.SS4.SSS1.p3.1.10" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">V1SCAN</span>, and the newly derived <span id="S4.SS4.SSS1.p3.1.11" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">LLM4SZZ+</span>. We evaluate three ensemble strategies as follows:

</div>

<div id="S4.SS4.SSS1.p4" class="ltx_para">

- <span id="S4.I4.i1"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_item">•</span></span>
  <div id="S4.I4.i1.p1" class="ltx_para">

  <span id="S4.I4.i1.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Inclusion Strategy.</span> The cumulative effect of integrating tools is evaluated by testing sizes 2 to 10 union sets.

  </div>
- <span id="S4.I4.i2"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_item">•</span></span>
  <div id="S4.I4.i2.p1" class="ltx_para">

  <span id="S4.I4.i2.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Voting Strategy.</span> To assess consensus-based robustness, we evaluate all combinations of 3, 5, 7, and 9 tools, marking a version as affected if the majority agrees.

  </div>
- <span id="S4.I4.i3"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_item">•</span></span>
  <div id="S4.I4.i3.p1" class="ltx_para">

  <span id="S4.I4.i3.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Best-in-Dimension Strategy.</span> We also select the best-performing tool in each of four key dimensions identified in RQ3 (e.g., patch modeling, commit tracing) and aggregate their outputs, leveraging their complementary strengths.

  </div>

</div>

<div id="S4.SS4.SSS2" class="section ltx_subsubsection">

#### <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsubsection"><span id="S4.SS4.SSS2.6" class="ltx_text">IV-D</span>2 </span><span id="S4.SS4.SSS2.7" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Results</span>

<div id="S4.SS4.SSS2.p1" class="ltx_para">

Table <a href="#S4.T6" class="ltx_ref" title="Table VI ‣ IV-D1 Setup ‣ IV-D Tool Combination Analysis (RQ4) ‣ IV Comparison and Evaluation ‣ Vulnerability-Affected Versions Identification: How Far Are We?"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">VI</span></a> summarizes the top-performing configurations under each combination strategy.

</div>

<div id="S4.SS4.SSS2.p2" class="ltx_para">

<span id="S4.SS4.SSS2.p2.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Modular Recomposition.</span> The best configuration, <span id="S4.SS4.SSS2.p2.1.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">LLM4SZZ+</span>, integrates LLM-based statement selection (S1), single-step blame tracing (S2), full-line coverage for commit tracing (S3), and cross-branch patch reuse (S4). This variant achieves 50.7% accuracy at the vulnerability level and 81.8% F1-score at the version level—outperforming the strongest tracing-based baseline (<span id="S4.SS4.SSS2.p2.1.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">VCCFinder</span>) by 5.8% and 4.0%, and the earlier hybrid version (<span id="S4.SS4.SSS2.p2.1.4" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">LLM4SZZ</span>) by 10.0% and 9.5%. Notably, replacing S3 component with LLM-selected commits significantly reduces performance (-7.3% accuracy, -5.7% F1), suggesting that though LLMs enhance early-stage selection, heuristic-based commit tracing remains more effective.

</div>

<div id="S4.SS4.SSS2.p3" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

<span class="ltx_inline-block"><img src="data:image/svg+xml;base64,PHN2ZyBpZD0iUzQuU1M0LlNTUzIucDMucGljMSIgY2xhc3M9Imx0eF9waWN0dXJlIiBoZWlnaHQ9IjY4Ljg5IiBvdmVyZmxvdz0idmlzaWJsZSIgdmVyc2lvbj0iMS4xIiB2aWV3Ym94PSIwIDAgNDc3LjM4IDY4Ljg5IiB3aWR0aD0iNDc3LjM4Ij48ZyBzdHlsZT0iLS1sdHgtc3Ryb2tlLWNvbG9yOiMwMDAwMDA7LS1sdHgtZmlsbC1jb2xvcjojMDAwMDAwOyIgZmlsbD0iIzAwMDAwMCIgc3Ryb2tlPSIjMDAwMDAwIiBzdHJva2Utd2lkdGg9IjAuNHB0IiB0cmFuc2Zvcm09InRyYW5zbGF0ZSgwLDY4Ljg5KSBtYXRyaXgoMSAwIDAgLTEgMCAwKSI+PGcgc3R5bGU9Ii0tbHR4LWZpbGwtY29sb3I6I0U2RTZFNjsiIGZpbGw9IiNFNkU2RTYiIGZpbGwtb3BhY2l0eT0iMS4wIj48cGF0aCBzdHlsZT0ic3Ryb2tlOm5vbmUiIGQ9Ik0gMS45NyA1LjkxIEwgMS45NyA2Mi45OSBDIDEuOTcgNjUuMTYgMy43MyA2Ni45MyA1LjkxIDY2LjkzIEwgNDcxLjQ3IDY2LjkzIEMgNDczLjY1IDY2LjkzIDQ3NS40MSA2NS4xNiA0NzUuNDEgNjIuOTkgTCA0NzUuNDEgNS45MSBDIDQ3NS40MSAzLjczIDQ3My42NSAxLjk3IDQ3MS40NyAxLjk3IEwgNS45MSAxLjk3IEMgMy43MyAxLjk3IDEuOTcgMy43MyAxLjk3IDUuOTEgWiIgLz48L2c+PGcgZmlsbC1vcGFjaXR5PSIxLjAiIHRyYW5zZm9ybT0ibWF0cml4KDEuMCAwLjAgMC4wIDEuMCA4Ljg5IDQuNzQpIj48Zm9yZWlnbm9iamVjdCBzdHlsZT0iLS1sdHgtZm8td2lkdGg6MzMuMjJlbTstLWx0eC1mby1oZWlnaHQ6NC4yOWVtOy0tbHR4LWZvLWRlcHRoOjBlbTtmb250LXNpemU6MTBwdDsiIGhlaWdodD0iNTkuNDIiIG92ZXJmbG93PSJ2aXNpYmxlIiB0cmFuc2Zvcm09Im1hdHJpeCgxIDAgMCAtMSAwIDU5LjQyKSIgd2lkdGg9IjQ1OS42NyI+PHNwYW4gY2xhc3M9Imx0eF9mb3JlaWdub2JqZWN0X2NvbnRhaW5lciI+PHNwYW4gY2xhc3M9Imx0eF9mb3JlaWdub2JqZWN0X2NvbnRlbnQiPgo8c3BhbiBpZD0iUzQuU1M0LlNTUzIucDMucGljMS4xIiBjbGFzcz0ibHR4X2lubGluZS1ibG9jayBsdHhfbWluaXBhZ2UgbHR4X2FsaWduX2JvdHRvbSIgc3R5bGU9IndpZHRoOjMzLjIyZW07Ij4KPHNwYW4gaWQ9IlM0LlNTNC5TU1MyLnAzLnBpYzEuMS4xIiBjbGFzcz0ibHR4X3AiPjxzcGFuIGlkPSJTNC5TUzQuU1NTMi5wMy5waWMxLjEuMS4xIiBjbGFzcz0ibHR4X3RleHQgbHR4X2ZvbnRfYm9sZCIgc3R5bGU9Ii0tbHR4LWZnLWNvbG9yOiMwMDAwMDA7Ij5GaW5kaW5nIDk6PHNwYW4gaWQ9IlM0LlNTNC5TU1MyLnAzLnBpYzEuMS4xLjEuMSIgY2xhc3M9Imx0eF90ZXh0IGx0eF9mb250X21lZGl1bSI+Ck1vZHVsYXIgcmVjb21wb3NpdGlvbiB5aWVsZHMgZ3JlYXQgaW1wcm92ZW1lbnQuIExMTXMgYXJlIGJlc3QgYXQKc3RhdGVtZW50IHNlbGVjdGlvbiwgd2hlcmVhcyBjb21taXQgaWRlbnRpZmljYXRpb24gYmVuZWZpdHMgbW9yZSBmcm9tIGhldXJpc3RpYyBtZXRob2RzLjwvc3Bhbj48L id="S4.SS4.SSS2.p3.pic1" class="ltx_picture" /></span>

</div>

<div id="S4.SS4.SSS2.p4" class="ltx_para">

<span id="S4.SS4.SSS2.p4.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Inclusion Strategy.</span> The combination of <span id="S4.SS4.SSS2.p4.1.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">ReDebug</span> and <span id="S4.SS4.SSS2.p4.1.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">LLM4SZZ+</span> achieves the highest accuracy at the vulnerability level (52.1%), an improvement of 7.2% over <span id="S4.SS4.SSS2.p4.1.4" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">VCCFinder</span> and 1.4% over <span id="S4.SS4.SSS2.p4.1.5" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">LLM4SZZ+</span>. At the version level, it reaches an F1-score of 84.0%. Another configuration—<span id="S4.SS4.SSS2.p4.1.6" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">ReDebug</span>, <span id="S4.SS4.SSS2.p4.1.7" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">V1SCAN</span>, and <span id="S4.SS4.SSS2.p4.1.8" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">LLM4SZZ+</span>—achieves the highest version-level F1-score (84.5%), outperforming <span id="S4.SS4.SSS2.p4.1.9" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">VCCFinder</span> and <span id="S4.SS4.SSS2.p4.1.10" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">LLM4SZZ+</span> by 6.7% and 2.7%, with a vulnerability-level accuracy of 51.7%.

</div>

<div id="S4.SS4.SSS2.p5" class="ltx_para">

<span id="S4.SS4.SSS2.p5.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Voting Strategy.</span> The optimal combination includes <span id="S4.SS4.SSS2.p5.1.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">V-SZZ</span>, <span id="S4.SS4.SSS2.p5.1.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">Lifetime</span>, <span id="S4.SS4.SSS2.p5.1.4" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">SEM-SZZ</span>, <span id="S4.SS4.SSS2.p5.1.5" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">Movery</span>, and <span id="S4.SS4.SSS2.p5.1.6" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">LLM4SZZ+</span>. This ensemble achieves 55.0% accuracy at the vulnerability level and 84.8% F1-score at the version level—improvements of 10.1% and 7.0% over <span id="S4.SS4.SSS2.p5.1.7" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">VCCFinder</span>, and 4.3% and 3.0% over <span id="S4.SS4.SSS2.p5.1.8" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">LLM4SZZ+</span>. The results highlight the effectiveness of majority voting in mitigating individual tool weaknesses.

</div>

<div id="S4.SS4.SSS2.p6" class="ltx_para">

<span id="S4.SS4.SSS2.p6.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Best-in-Dimension Strategy.</span> Focusing on patch modification types (Add-only, Del-only, Mixed), the combination of <span id="S4.SS4.SSS2.p6.1.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">SEM-SZZ</span>, <span id="S4.SS4.SSS2.p6.1.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">LLM4SZZ</span>, and <span id="S4.SS4.SSS2.p6.1.4" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">V-SZZ</span> achieves 50.3% accuracy at the vulnerability level and 81.7% F1-score at the version level—surpassing <span id="S4.SS4.SSS2.p6.1.5" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">VCCFinder</span> by 5.4% and 3.9%. While marginally below <span id="S4.SS4.SSS2.p6.1.6" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">LLM4SZZ+</span>, it shows the value of aligning tool selection with orthogonal performance dimensions.

</div>

<div id="S4.SS4.SSS2.p7" class="ltx_para">

In summary, the experimental results across all three strategies consistently demonstrate the benefits of tool combination. First, ensemble methods provide substantial improvements over the best standalone and hybrid tools, confirming the overall effectiveness of tool integration. Second, the top-performing combinations in the Inclusion and Voting strategies consistently integrate both tracing-based and matching-based approaches, validating their complementary strengths. Third, <span id="S4.SS4.SSS2.p7.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">LLM4SZZ+</span> is included in all leading combinations, underscoring its robustness as a foundation for ensemble configurations. However, the best vulnerability-level accuracy remains below 60%, reflecting fundamental limits in existing tool architectures—such as reliance on heuristics, insufficient semantic modeling, and coarse-grained decision logic.

</div>

<div id="S4.SS4.SSS2.p8" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

<div id="S5" class="section ltx_section">

## <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_section">V </span><span id="S5.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">Discussion</span>

<div id="S5.SS1" class="section ltx_subsection">

### <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsection"><span id="S5.SS1.6" class="ltx_text">V-A</span> </span><span id="S5.SS1.7" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">Challenges and Implications</span>

<div id="S5.SS1.p1" class="ltx_para">

Our study reveals that existing tools for vulnerability-affected version identification suffer from substantial limitations across three dimensions: ❶ noisy and coarse-grained patches, ❷ semantic mismatch between fix locations and root causes, and ❸ shallow presence verification. These issues span diff-based heuristics (e.g., <span id="S5.SS1.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">V-SZZ</span>), semantic analysis (e.g., <span id="S5.SS1.p1.1.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">MOVERY</span>), and LLM-based approaches (e.g., <span id="S5.SS1.p1.1.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">LLM4SZZ</span>).

</div>

<div id="S5.SS1.p2" class="ltx_para">

<span id="S5.SS1.p2.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Noisy patches.</span> Most tools assume that any changes in a vulnerability patch are relevant. However, patches frequently contain unrelated edits, such as refactoring or multi-issue fixes, which impair downstream analysis. Our manual inspection indicates that 19% of patches contain unrelated changes. Despite this, few tools perform effective preprocessing to filter noise. Although <span id="S5.SS1.p2.1.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">LLM4SZZ</span> attempts to isolate relevant edits using large language models, its failure rate remains 35%, highlighting this task’s difficulty.

</div>

<div id="S5.SS1.p3" class="ltx_para">

<span id="S5.SS1.p3.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Semantic mismatch.</span> A more fundamental limitation arises from the disconnect between patch locations and vulnerability root causes. Certain vulnerability types (e.g., <span id="S5.SS1.p3.1.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">double free</span>, <span id="S5.SS1.p3.1.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">use-after-free</span>) involve complex inter-procedural interactions, with contributing code located in different functions or files. Even within a single function, control-flow-preserving changes (e.g., replacing <span id="S5.SS1.p3.1.4" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">return</span> with <span id="S5.SS1.p3.1.5" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">goto</span> for cleanup) can lead to incorrect identification when tools lack flow sensitivity. In our study, <span id="S5.SS1.p3.1.6" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">SEM-SZZ</span> and <span id="S5.SS1.p3.1.7" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">MOVERY</span> yielded 39 and 47 false positives, respectively, when identifying vulnerability-related statements across 100 samples.

</div>

<div id="S5.SS1.p4" class="ltx_para">

<span id="S5.SS1.p4.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Simplistic presence verification.</span> Existing tools often rely on tracing heuristics or feature-matching strategies to determine whether a version is vulnerable. Tracing-based methods are prone to misidentifying vulnerability-introducing commits, while feature-matching approaches focus on syntactic similarity without verifying whether the vulnerability condition persists. The absence of root-cause-aware reasoning leads to both high false-positive and false-negative rates.

</div>

</div>

<div id="S5.SS2" class="section ltx_subsection">

### <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsection"><span id="S5.SS2.6" class="ltx_text">V-B</span> </span><span id="S5.SS2.7" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">Practical Workarounds via Tool Combination</span>

<div id="S5.SS2.p1" class="ltx_para">

Given that no individual tool achieves more than 44.9% accuracy or 77.8% F1-score, we explore ensemble strategies as practical alternatives. A majority-voting approach across five tools improves accuracy and F1-score by 10.1% and 7.0%, respectively, over the best standalone method. The finding demonstrates that tools offer complementary strengths, and that combination helps offset single tool’s limitations. Though not a substitute for deeper technical advances, such ensembles provide a viable interim solution in practice.

</div>

</div>

<div id="S5.SS3" class="section ltx_subsection">

### <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsection"><span id="S5.SS3.6" class="ltx_text">V-C</span> </span><span id="S5.SS3.7" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">Future Directions</span>

<div id="S5.SS3.p1" class="ltx_para">

To address the above limitations more fundamentally, we highlight three promising research directions:

</div>

<div id="S5.SS3.p2" class="ltx_para">

<span id="S5.SS3.p2.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold ltx_font_italic">1) Patch preprocessing.</span> Future approaches should move beyond treating patches as atomic units. Combining static analysis with LLMs could enable finer-grained filtering of unrelated edits. Patch modularization—partitioning edits by functional or semantic unit—may further improve the mapping between fixes and individual vulnerabilities.

</div>

<div id="S5.SS3.p3" class="ltx_para">

<span id="S5.SS3.p3.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold ltx_font_italic">2) Root cause localization.</span> While root cause analysis remains difficult when limited to patches, progress can be made by designing analysis strategies tailored to specific vulnerability types and remediation patterns. Additionally, prompting LLMs with structured expert knowledge—e.g., through chain-of-thought reasoning or multi-role dialogue—may help elicit more accurate vulnerability semantics.

</div>

<div id="S5.SS3.p4" class="ltx_para">

<span id="S5.SS3.p4.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold ltx_font_italic">3) Presence verification.</span> As code evolves, the boundary between fixed and vulnerable states becomes blurred. We advocate for verification strategies grounded in vulnerability semantics, ideally aligned with identified root causes. Few-shot prompting, multi-agent validation, and root-cause-aware reasoning are promising techniques to improve robustness and reduce both false positives and false negatives.

</div>

</div>

<div id="S5.SS4" class="section ltx_subsection">

### <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsection"><span id="S5.SS4.6" class="ltx_text">V-D</span> </span><span id="S5.SS4.7" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">Threats to Validity</span>

<div id="S5.SS4.SSS1" class="section ltx_subsubsection">

#### <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsubsection"><span id="S5.SS4.SSS1.6" class="ltx_text">V-D</span>1 </span><span id="S5.SS4.SSS1.7" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">External Validity</span>

<div id="S5.SS4.SSS1.p1" class="ltx_para">

Our study focuses on C/C++ open-source projects, which may limit the generalizability to other languages and ecosystems. Nonetheless, C/C++ is central to memory-related vulnerabilities, the main focus of existing tools. To ensure coverage, we selected nine actively maintained projects from diverse domains (e.g., OSs, databases, web servers). While our dataset includes 1,128 vulnerabilities, it may underrepresent rare or poorly documented types. We mitigated this by validating coverage against the CWE Top 25, though long-tail cases may still be missing.

</div>

</div>

<div id="S5.SS4.SSS2" class="section ltx_subsubsection">

#### <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsubsection"><span id="S5.SS4.SSS2.6" class="ltx_text">V-D</span>2 </span><span id="S5.SS4.SSS2.7" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Internal Validity</span>

<div id="S5.SS4.SSS2.p1" class="ltx_para">

Manual labeling of ground truth may introduce error, but it is necessary due to the absence of standard benchmarks. We mitigated this via double annotation by experienced reviewers, with conflicts resolved by a third expert. While tool selection may miss obscure methods, we combined comprehensive keyword searches with multi-round snowballing, covering all major technique categories and emphasizing tools with public implementations. Tools were run with default configurations, which may affect fairness; however, these typically reflect optimal settings as reported. Lastly, manual FP/FN inspection may involve bias; to reduce this, we used standardized criteria and cross-checking, with minimal disagreements resolved through discussion.

</div>

</div>

</div>

</div>

<div id="S6" class="section ltx_section">

## <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_section">VI </span><span id="S6.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">Conclusion</span>

<div id="S6.p1" class="ltx_para">

We conducted the first comprehensive empirical study on vulnerability-affected versions identification, evaluating 12 tools across 1,128 real-world C/C++ vulnerabilities. Our results show that no tool achieves over 45.0% accuracy, largely due to heuristic reliance, limited semantic understanding, and inflexible matching strategies. Even with ensemble methods, accuracy remains below 60.0%, indicating fundamental limitations in existing designs. Our benchmark and analysis provide practical guidance for tool development and establish a foundation for future work toward more accurate and resilient approaches.

</div>

</div>

Our team has already identified <a href="https://github.com/arXiv/html_feedback/issues" class="ltx_ref" target="_blank">the following issues</a>. We appreciate your time reviewing and reporting rendering errors we may not have found yet. Your efforts will help us improve the HTML versions for all readers, because disability should not be a barrier to accessing research. Thank you for your continued support in championing open access for all.

Have a free development cycle? Help support accessibility at arXiv!
